S<sub>em</sub>i<sub>-superv</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d ti<sub>me ser</sub>i<sub>es anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> TSAD <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e sem</sub>i<sub>-superv</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d <sub>se</sub>tti<sub>ng</sub> h<sub>as</sub> b<sub>een</sub> approached from several broad angles. Reconstruction-based methods train a model on anomaly-free data and score anomalies b<sub>y</sub> reconstruction error: USAD (Audibert et al., 2020) introduces adversarial training of paired autoencoders; Anomaly Transformer (Xu et al., 2022) exploits the asymmetric priorassociation / series-association gap within a single-scale Transformer; DCdetector (Yang et al., 2023) contrasts dual patch-attention views of the same series. Forecasting- andfrequency-based methods instead score deviations from <sub>p</sub>redicted or fre<sub>q</sub>uenc<sub>y</sub>-domain normal <sub>p</sub>atterns: TimesNet (Wu et al., 2023) converts 1-D series into 2-D tensors alon<sub>g</sub> dominant <sub>p</sub>eriods; FITS (Xu et al., 2024) models time series with a li<sub>g</sub>htwei<sub>g</sub>ht fre<sub>q</sub>uenc<sub>y</sub> inter<sub>p</sub>olation module; CATCH (Wu et al., 2025) introduces channel-aware fre<sub>q</sub>uenc<sub>y p</sub>atchin<sub>g</sub> for multivariate anomal<sub>y</sub> detection; KAN-AD (Zhou et al., 2025) uses Fourier-based Kolmogorov–Arnold networks to model smooth temporal patterns with few parameters. Recent general and representation-based detectors further improve robustness and transferability: DADA (Shentu et al., 2025) <sub>p</sub>re-trains a <sub>g</sub>eneral anomal<sub>y</sub> detector with ada<sub>p</sub>tive bottlenecks and dual adversarial decoders, while PaAno (Park & Kan<sub>g</sub>, 2026) learns <sub>p</sub>atch-level re<sub>p</sub>resentations with a li<sub>g</sub>htwei<sub>g</sub>ht 1D-CNN and <sub>scores</sub> <sub>anoma</sub>li<sub>es</sub> b<sub>y</sub> <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>norma</sub>l t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es.</sub>

M<sub>u</sub>lti<sub>-sca</sub>l<sub>e anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ors.</sub> Th<sub>e na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l <sub>response</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e granu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub>it<sub>y</sub> dil<sub>emma</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o run</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> <sub>a</sub>t <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e sca</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> E<sub>x</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ng mu</sub>lti<sub>-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gns</sub> f<sub>or</sub> TSAD f<sub>a</sub>ll i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> t<sub>wo camps,</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t from ours. (i) Scale-independent parallel branches. MTST (Zhang et al., 2024b) and ElasTST (Zhang et al., 2024a) tokenize at multi<sub>p</sub>le <sub>p</sub>atch sizes but <sub>p</sub>rocess each scale inde<sub>p</sub>endentl<sub>y</sub> and concatenate features without cross-scale attention; TimesNet (Wu et al., 2023) a<sub>pp</sub>lies <sub>p</sub>eriod-s<sub>p</sub>ecific 2-D convolution and <sub>recom</sub>bi<sub>nes on</sub>l<sub>y v</sub>i<sub>a amp</sub>lit<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>e</sub>d <sub>summa</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> N<sub>one o</sub>f th<sub>ese a</sub>ll<sub>ow one sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub>’<sub>s represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>e</sub> sha ed b another’s within the backbone. (ii) Asymmetric hierarchies. Besides CrossAD (Li et al., 2025), Pathformer (Chen et al., 2024) routes forecastin<sub>g</sub> information across scales via an ada<sub>p</sub>tive <sub>p</sub>athwa<sub>y g</sub>ate; Scaleformer and coarse-to-fine <sub>py</sub>ramidal Transformers (Shabani et al., 2023; Wu et al., 2024) refine <sub>p</sub>redictions hierarchicall<sub>y</sub>; TimeMixer (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2024) mixes <sub>p</sub>ast and future decom<sub>p</sub>ositions with directed (non-s<sub>y</sub>mmetric) flows. All share the same <sub>p</sub>rivile<sub>g</sub>ed-scale inductive bias. ## 3.2 Framework Overview

Th<sub>e core o</sub>f MSCAD i<sub>s</sub> it<sub>s recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l $g _ { \boldsymbol { \theta } . }$ <sub>, a mu</sub>lti<sub>-sca</sub>l<sub>e au</sub>t<sub>oenco</sub>d<sub>er w</sub>ith <sub>symme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> bidi<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>cross-sca</sub>l<sub>e commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Gi<sub>ven an</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub> $\mathbf { x } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { W \times C }$ <sub>,</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> th<sub>e w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow-</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y score</sub> $f _ { \boldsymbol { \theta } } ( \mathbf { x } )$ end-to-end in the followin<sub>g</sub> three sta<sub>g</sub>es (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 2). ① Per-scale encodin<sub>g</sub>. The window is processed in parallel by S scale branches indexed by a fixed set of patch sizes $\mathcal { P } =$ $\{ P _ { 1 } , P _ { 2 } , \dots , P _ { S } \}$ (we use $S = 3$ <sub>an</sub>d $\dot { \mathcal { P } } = \{ 4 , 1 6 , 6 4 \}$ throughout the paper). Each branch Branch<sub>s</sub> tokenizes x into $N _ { s }$ <sub>pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens v</sub>i<sub>a a s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>processes</sub> th<sub>em w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> T<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ormer</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er, pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng per-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\mathbf { z } _ { s } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { N _ { s } \times \mathbf { \hat { d } } }$ (§3.3). ② S<sub>y</sub>mmetric cross-scale brid<sub>g</sub>e. The <sub>p</sub>er-scale l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\{ \bar { \mathbf { z } } _ { s } \} _ { s = 1 } ^ { S }$ pass through a stack of B bridge modules that lets each scale’s tokens attend to the <sub>conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er sca</sub>l<sub>es, y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ldi<sub>ng con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>-m</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>d l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s</sub> $\{ \tilde { \mathbf { z } } _ { s } \} _ { s = 1 } ^ { S }$ <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e same per-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> shapes. The bridge is symmetric (i.e., no scale is privileged as a conditioning signal) and bidirectional $( i . e . ,$ ever<sub>y p</sub>air of scales both informs and is informed b<sub>y</sub> the other), which is the core architectural de<sub>p</sub>arture from <sub>p</sub>rior multi-scale TSAD (§3.4). ③ Per-scale decodin<sub>g</sub> and uniform fusion. Each brid<sub>g</sub>ed latent $\tilde { \mathbf { z } } _ { s }$ is projected back to the patch space by a linear decoder, yielding a per-scale window-level reconstruction error $e _ { s } ( \mathbf { x } ) \in \mathbb { R } _ { \geq 0 }$ , the mean squared error between the reconstructed and original patches at scale s $( \ S 3 . 3 )$ . The S per-scale errors are fused into the final window-level anomaly score by a simple uniform a<sup>v</sup>e<sup>r</sup>ag<sup>in</sup>g $\begin{array} { r } { f _ { \theta } ( \mathbf { \bar { x } } ) ~ = ~ \frac { 1 } { S } \sum _ { s = 1 } ^ { S } e _ { s } ( \mathbf { x } ) } \end{array}$ , which also serves as the <sub>p</sub>er-window trainin<sub>g</sub> loss (§3.5).

Each of the three sta<sub>g</sub>es ma<sub>p</sub>s directl<sub>y</sub> to the <sub>g</sub>a<sub>p</sub> dia<sub>g</sub>nosed in §1: <sub>p</sub>arallel branches <sub>p</sub>reserve scale-s<sub>p</sub>ecific <sub>recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>s so no sca</sub>l<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>sor</sub>b<sub>s ano</sub>th<sub>er</sub>’<sub>s s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e symme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge ena</sub>bl<sub>es</sub> bidi<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> fl<sub>ow w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>a coarse-</sub>t<sub>o-</sub>fi<sub>ne asymme</sub>t<sub>ry, an</sub>d <sub>un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub> f<sub>us</sub>i<sub>on esc</sub>h<sub>ews</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d routin<sub>g</sub>, which we find redundant once the brid<sub>g</sub>e has alread<sub>y</sub> reconciled semantics across scales (§4.3).

The cross-scale brid<sub>g</sub>e is a unified module <sub>p</sub>ositioned between the <sub>p</sub>er-scale encoders and decoders (§3.3). Cross-attention block. For each scale s, we form a context sequence by concatenating the latents of <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er sca</sub>l<sub>es,</sub> $\mathbf { C } _ { s } = \operatorname { c o n c a t } ( \mathbf { z } _ { j } : j \neq s ) \in \mathbb { R } ^ { ( \sum _ { j \neq s } N _ { j } ) \times d }$ <sub>, an</sub>d <sub>app</sub>l<sub>y mu</sub>lti<sub>-</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>cross-a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> ${ \bf a } _ { s } =$ $\mathrm { M H A } ( Q = \mathbf { z } _ { s } , \ K = \mathbf { C } _ { s } , \ V = \mathbf { C } _ { s } ) \in \mathbb { R } ^ { N _ { s } \times d }$ . The output is updated via a standard Transformer blocks <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>connec</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d L<sub>ayer</sub>N<sub>orm:</sub> $\mathbf { z } _ { s } ^ { \prime } = \mathrm { L a y e r N o r m } ( \hat { \mathbf { z } } _ { s } + \mathbf { a } _ { s } ) , \quad \tilde { \mathbf { z } } _ { s } = \mathrm { L a y e r N o r m } ( \mathbf { z } _ { s } ^ { \prime } + \mathrm { F F N } ( \mathbf { z } _ { s } ^ { \prime } ) )$ All attention projections are shared across scales, and the bridge consists of $B = 2$ <sub>suc</sub>h bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s.</sub>

S <sub>mme</sub>t<sub>r an</sub>d bidi<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>lit <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id <sub>e</sub> i<sub>s s mme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c: no sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s r</sub>i<sub>v</sub>il<sub>e e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e o era</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> invariant under any joint permutation of the scale indices on inputs and outputs. It is also bidirectional, in that every pair of scales informs the other within a single forward pass: scale s queries scale j through $\mathbf { C } _ { s } ,$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> $j$ simultaneously queries scale s through $\mathbf { C } _ { j }$ <sub>, w</sub>ith b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> h<sub>appen</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>e</sub> decoder is called. ## 4 Ex<sub>p</sub>eriments and Results

## 4.1 Ex<sub>p</sub>erimental Desi<sub>g</sub>n

Benchmark and <sub>p</sub>rotocol. We evaluate on TSB-AD (Liu & Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos, 2024), the most com<sub>p</sub>rehensive <sub>pu</sub>bli<sub>c</sub> TSAD b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> E<sub>ar</sub>li<sub>er</sub> TSAD <sub>researc</sub>h <sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>di<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>aroun</sub>d <sub>a</sub> h<sub>an</sub>df<sub>u</sub>l <sub>o</sub>f <sub>rea</sub>l<sub>-wor</sub>ld multivariate datasets (SMAP and MSL (Hundman et al., 2018), SMD (Su et al., 2019), SWaT and WADI (Goh et al., 2017), PSM (Abdulaal et al., 2021)) and a <sub>g</sub>rowin<sub>g</sub> suite of univariate collections (UCR Anomal<sub>y</sub> Archive (Wu & Keo<sub>g</sub>h, 2023), NAB, Yahoo); TSB-AD consolidates this settin<sub>g</sub> under a unified evaluation protocol. The oficial evaluation split contains 350 univariate (TSB-AD-U) and 180 multivariate (TSB-AD-M) series spanning 40 datasets across healthcare, networking, IoT, server <sub>mon</sub>it<sub>or</sub>i<sub>ng, an</sub>d <sub>syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>es</sub> i<sub>s par</sub>titi<sub>one</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng pre</sub>fi<sub>x an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>su</sub>fi<sub>x;</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k’<sub>s sem</sub>i<sub>-superv</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>, every</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>score</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>es a</sub>t t<sub>es</sub>t ti<sub>me, w</sub>ith <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d <sub>su</sub>fi<sub>x.</sub> M<sub>u</sub>lti<sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> series are processed channel-independently and per-channel scores are averaged, following the TSB-AD <sub>conven</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> F<sub>ur</sub>th<sub>er</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s o</sub>f TSB<sub>-</sub>AD <sub>are</sub> li<sub>s</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> A<sub>.</sub>

Metrics. Our <sub>p</sub>rimar<sub>y</sub> metric is volume-under-the-surface <sub>p</sub>recision–recall (VUS-PR) (Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos et al., 2022), which inte<sub>g</sub>rates <sub>p</sub>recision–recall over all detection thresholds and a small tem<sub>p</sub>oral bufer; unlike Point-Adjusted F1 (PA-F1) it is threshold-free and not gameable by random scores. Point-Adjusted F1 was long commonly used in TSAD evaluation, but Kim et al. (2022) show that random scores can exceed 0.70 PA-F1 on most standard datasets. For com<sub>p</sub>leteness we additionall<sub>y</sub> re<sub>p</sub>ort VUS-ROC<sub>,</sub> AUC-PR<sub>,</sub> AUC-ROC<sub>,</sub> and two F1 variants, Ran<sub>g</sub>e-based F1 and standard Point-F1, all from the TSB-AD metric suite (Liu & Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos, 2024), to characterize behaviour under diferent detection re<sub>g</sub>imes. PA-F1, to<sub>g</sub>ether with E<sub>ven</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d<sub>-</sub>F1 <sub>an</sub>d Afili<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>F<sub>, are repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> B<sub>.</sub> U<sub>n</sub>i<sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e means are</sub> re<sub>p</sub>orted se<sub>p</sub>aratel<sub>y</sub> throu<sub>g</sub>hout<sub>; p</sub>er-dataset breakdowns a<sub>pp</sub>ear in A<sub>pp</sub>endix E.

2025), KAN-AD (Zhou et al., 2025)), recent re<sub>p</sub>resentation or <sub>g</sub>eneral TSAD models (DADA (Shentu et al., 2025), PaAno (Park & Kan<sub>g</sub>, 2026)), multi-scale TSAD via CrossAD (Li et al., 2025), and foundation/<sub>p</sub>retrained models (OFA (Zhou et al., 2023), La<sub>g</sub>-Llama (Rasul et al., 2024), MOMENT (Goswami et al., 2024), TimesFM (Das et al., 2024)). Full baseline introductions is <sub>p</sub>rovided in A<sub>pp</sub>endix C.

Implementation. Unless otherwise noted, MSCAD uses window length W = 128, patch sizes P = {4, 16, 64}, token width d = 256, L = 2 Transformer encoder layers per branch with h = 4 heads, and a stack of B = 2 symmetric cross-scale attention bridge blocks. Training uses Adam with learning rate 10<sup>−3</sup>, batch size 128, cosine schedule over E = 30 epochs, gradient-norm clipping at 1.0, and early stopping with patience 5 on a 10% random validation hold-out. The same configuration is applied to every series, with no per-file hyperparameter tuning. Each MSCAD result is the mean over 3 seeds on the headline <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> F<sub>u</sub>ll i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s are</sub> i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> D<sub>.</sub>

## 4.2 Main Results

Table 1 reports MSCAD on six metrics on the univariate and multivariate splits of TSB-AD against 18 and 16 representative baselines, respectively. MSCAD is the top method on every reported metric on both splits. On the primary metric VUS-PR, MSCAD reaches 0.57 on UTS, a +0.05 absolute / +9.6% relative improvement over the strongest baseline, PaAno (2026), and 0.47 on MTS, a +0.04 absolute / +9.3% relative im<sub>p</sub>rovement over the stron<sub>g</sub>est multivariate baseline, PaAno (2026). The <sub>g</sub>ains are es<sub>p</sub>eciall<sub>y</sub> clear on the precision-recall metrics: MSCAD improves AUC-PR by +0.08 on UTS and +0.07 on MTS, and improves VUS-PR by +0.05 and +0.04, respectively. ## 4<sub>.</sub>3 Model Anal<sub>y</sub>sis

We run four controlled ablations at the headline configuration (d=256, W=128, S=3, B=2, attention bridge), reported as 3-seed mean±stddev across seeds {2026, 2027, 2028} on VUS-PR. The first two i<sub>so</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e ro</sub>l<sub>e o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> thi<sub>r</sub>d <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>rms</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>dli<sub>ne num</sub>b<sub>ers are no</sub>t th<sub>e resu</sub>lt <sub>o</sub>f HP <sub>over-</sub>fitti<sub>ng;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>our</sub>th <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>-a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>ti<sub>ve rou</sub>ti<sub>ng on</sub> t<sub>op o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge</sub> i<sub>s unnecessary.</sub>

Bridge ablation. Table 2 compares three regimes: no bridge at all, an identity bridge that runs the FFN+residual block but injects zero cross-scale context, and the symmetric cross-attention bridge at depth B=2. The identity variant lifts All by only +0.002 over no-bridge, within seed noise, demonstrating th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> FFN+<sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>wrapper</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>one</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>ner</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Addi<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>symme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>cross-a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>ayers</sub> lift<sub>s</sub> All b<sub>y</sub> +0.017, confirming that the cross-scale exchange (not the wrapper) is the load-bearing component.

S<sub>ymme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c vs. asymme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> fl<sub>ow.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 3 <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>aces</sub> th<sub>e symme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c exc</sub>h<sub>ange w</sub>ith di<sub>rec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> fl<sub>ows a</sub>t the same <sub>p</sub>arameter count. In the CrossAD-st<sub>y</sub>le coarse-to-fine variant (c2f), each scale attends onl<sub>y</sub> to <sub>coarser sca</sub>l<sub>es, w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e coarses</sub>t <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e rece</sub>i<sub>ves an em</sub> t <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>s asse</sub>d th<sub>rou</sub> h th<sub>e</sub> FFN+<sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l block. This drops Average VUS-PR from 0.5356 to 0.5112 (−0.0244), retaining only a small fraction of the gain over the no-bridge baseline (0.5051 in Table 2). The fine-to-coarse variant (f2c) performs similarly to no-bridge at 0.5041. These directed designs restrict reciprocal exchange: c2f prevents coarse-scale <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>query</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> fi<sub>ne-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>o</sub>ft<sub>en</sub> <sub>carry</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>-</sub>f<sub>requency</sub> <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>f</sub>2c d<sub>epr</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> fi<sub>ne-sca</sub>l<sub>e represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons o</sub>f b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>er con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e resu</sub>lt <sub>sugges</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t MSCAD’<sub>s ga</sub>i<sub>n comes</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> bidi<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>on, no</sub>t <sub>mere</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>rom a</sub>ddi<sub>ng cross-a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on parame</sub>t<sub>ers.</sub>

H<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>er sens</sub>iti<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 13 <sub>sweeps</sub> th<sub>ree o</sub>f th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> HP<sub>s aroun</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>dli<sub>ne.</sub> T<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en w</sub>idth saturates at d=256 (the next step d=512 regresses by −0.002, indicating that the larger width adds noise on the relativel<sub>y</sub> short trainin<sub>g p</sub>refixes rather than ca<sub>p</sub>turin<sub>g</sub> additional si<sub>g</sub>nal). Brid<sub>g</sub>e de<sub>p</sub>th saturates at B=2: depth-3 and depth-4 both regress, suggesting the bridge over-mixes scales when stacked too deeply. Window size is inverted-U with a peak at W=128: narrower windows lose long-context anomaly <sub>coverage; w</sub>id<sub>er w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ows s</sub>hift th<sub>e va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f th<sub>e per-w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow norma</sub>l <sub>man</sub>if<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>an</sub>d degrade early-stopping. The 3-scale patch set {4, 16, 64} is the default; the scale-set composition sweep is in A<sub>pp</sub>endix F.

Fusion gate. Replacing the uniform 1/S aggregator with a learned MLP gate over four per-window statistics does not improve VUS-PR: the all-feature gate reaches 0.5346, the random-feature control reaches 0.5347, and individual feature-drop variants fall in 0.5312–0.5349. The uniform default reaches 0.5356, suggesting that once the symmetric bridge has reconciled scale semantics, an input-adaptive <sub>rou</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>ayer</sub> <sub>a</sub>dd<sub>s</sub> <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>a</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>measura</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>.</sub> F<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub>bl<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>are</sub> i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> F<sub>.</sub>

It reports <sup>f</sup>our t<sup>h</sup>res<sup>h</sup>o<sup>ld</sup>-<sup>f</sup>ree metr<sup>i</sup>cs: AUC-PR, AUC-ROC, VUS-PR, an<sup>d</sup> VUS-ROC. It also reports thresholded F1 variants, including standard point-F1, point-adjusted F1, eventbased F1, ran<sub>g</sub>e-based F1, and afiliation-F. Followin<sub>g</sub> TSB-AD and the VUS metric <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osal (Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos et al., 2022), we use VUS-PR as the <sub>p</sub>rimar<sub>y</sub> metric. VUS evaluates detection <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> over a ran<sub>g</sub>e of th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>s an</sub>d t<sub>empora</sub>l t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ows, w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on-reca</sub>ll <sub>v</sub>i<sub>ew</sub> i<sub>s more sens</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> th<sub>an</sub> ROC t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>rare-pos</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>me</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> t<sub>rea</sub>t VUS<sub>-</sub>PR <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>mary</sub> <sub>ran</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> <sub>cr</sub>it<sub>er</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>repor</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er</sub> <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>suppor</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence.</sub>

## B Evaluation Metrics

Thi<sub>s appen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on measures use</sub>d i<sub>n our</sub> TSB<sub>-</sub>AD <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l TSB-AD metr<sup>i</sup>c <sup>i</sup>nter<sup>f</sup>ace get\_metrics(score, labels) (L<sup>i</sup>u & Paparr<sup>i</sup>zos, 2024), w<sup>hi</sup>c<sup>h</sup> ta<sup>k</sup>es a <sub>rea</sub>l<sub>-va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub>d <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y score an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> bi<sub>nary groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>sequence</sub> f<sub>or eac</sub>h ti<sub>me ser</sub>i<sub>es.</sub> L<sub>arger scores</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e more anoma</sub>l<sub>ous</sub> ti<sub>mes</sub>t<sub>amps.</sub> U<sub>n</sub>l<sub>ess s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se, eac</sub>h <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>s compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or eac</sub>h ti<sub>me ser</sub>i<sub>es an</sub>d th<sub>en average</sub>d <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on sp</sub>lit<sub>; we</sub> d<sub>o no</sub>t <sub>poo</sub>l ti<sub>mes</sub>t<sub>amps</sub> across datasets before computing a metric. All metrics are reported in [0, 1], and larger values are better.

## B<sub>.</sub>1 S<sub>core an</sub>d L<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l C<sub>onven</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>

F<sub>or a</sub> ti<sub>me ser</sub>i<sub>es o</sub>f l<sub>eng</sub>th $T ,$ l<sub>e</sub>t $y = ( y _ { 1 } , \dots , y _ { T } ) \in \{ 0 , 1 \} ^ { T }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $y _ { t } = 1$ means timestamp t belongs to an anomalous region. Let $s = \bar { ( \ l _ { 1 } , \ l _ { 1 } , \ l _ { 1 } , \ l _ { 2 } , \ l _ { 3 _ { T } } ) } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { T }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> detector’s anomaly score. A threshold θ induces binary predictions

$$
{ \hat { y } } _ { t } ( \theta ) = \mathbf { 1 } [ s _ { t } > \theta ] .\tag{3}
$$

Threshold-independent metrics evaluate the ranking induced by s over all thresholds. Thresholdd<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub> <sub>a</sub> bi<sub>nary</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> <sub>no</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld i<sub>s</sub> <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> TSB<sub>-</sub>AD i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>e-</sub> <sub>men</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on repor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue over an orac</sub>l<sub>e</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>searc</sub>h<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore use</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t F1 <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s on</sub>l<sub>y as</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>ti<sub>c secon</sub>d<sub>ary me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs, no</sub>t <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>mary ran</sub>ki<sub>ng cr</sub>it<sub>er</sub>i<sub>on.</sub>

## B<sub>.</sub>2 Th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>-</sub>I<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t P<sub>o</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t M<sub>e</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs</sub>

AUC<sub>-</sub>ROC<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e area un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e rece</sub>i<sub>ver opera</sub>ti<sub>ng c</sub>h<sub>arac</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c curve measures</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>d<sub>eo</sub>f b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> true-<sub>p</sub>ositive rate and false-<sub>p</sub>ositive rate as the threshold varies (Fawcett, 2006):

$$
\mathrm { T P R } ( \theta ) = \frac { \sum _ { t } \mathbf { 1 } [ \hat { y } _ { t } ( \theta ) = 1 \wedge y _ { t } = 1 ] } { \sum _ { t } \mathbf { 1 } [ y _ { t } = 1 ] } , \qquad \mathrm { F P R } ( \theta ) = \frac { \sum _ { t } \mathbf { 1 } [ \hat { y } _ { t } ( \theta ) = 1 \wedge y _ { t } = 0 ] } { \sum _ { t } \mathbf { 1 } [ y _ { t } = 0 ] } .\tag{4}
$$

TSB-AD computes AUC-ROC w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup> sklearn.metrics.roc\_auc\_score. T<sup>hi</sup>s metr<sup>i</sup>c <sup>i</sup>s use<sup>f</sup>u<sup>l</sup> <sup>f</sup>or ran<sup>k</sup>- i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e</sub> <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>severe</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub> i<sub>m</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ance</sub> b<sub>ecause</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se-pos</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>arge</sub> <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>norma</sub>l ti<sub>mes</sub>t<sub>amps.</sub>

AUC-PR. The area under the <sub>p</sub>recision-recall curve uses

$$
\operatorname { P r e c i s i o n } ( \theta ) = \frac { \sum _ { t } \mathbf { 1 } [ \hat { y } _ { t } ( \theta ) = 1 \wedge y _ { t } = 1 ] } { \sum _ { t } \mathbf { 1 } [ \hat { y } _ { t } ( \theta ) = 1 ] } , \qquad \operatorname { R e c a l l } ( \theta ) = \operatorname { T P R } ( \theta ) .\tag{5}
$$

TSB-AD computes AUC-PR as average prec<sup>i</sup>s<sup>i</sup>on w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup> sklearn.metrics.average\_precision\_ score. Prec<sup>i</sup>s<sup>i</sup>on-reca<sup>ll</sup> curves are common<sup>l</sup>y use<sup>d</sup> a<sup>l</sup>ongs<sup>id</sup>e ROC curves <sup>f</sup>or ran<sup>k</sup>e<sup>d bi</sup>nary eva<sup>l</sup>uation (Davis & Goadrich, 2006). Because <sub>p</sub>recision directl<sub>y p</sub>enalizes false alarms amon<sub>g p</sub>redicted anomalies, AUC-PR is more informative than AUC-ROC in rare-anomal<sub>y</sub> re<sub>g</sub>imes (Saito & Rehmsmeier, 2015). However, both AUC-ROC and AUC-PR are <sub>p</sub>oint-wise: the<sub>y</sub> treat each timestam<sub>p</sub> inde<sub>p</sub>endentl<sub>y</sub> <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>o no</sub>t <sub>accoun</sub>t f<sub>or sma</sub>ll t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>s</sub>hift<sub>s</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erva</sub>l<sub>s.</sub>

VUS extends <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> AUC b<sub>y</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> <sub>over</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>axes:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>score</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance</sub> <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on a</sub>dd<sub>resses a common proper</sub>t<sub>y o</sub>f TSAD l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s: a</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or may reac</sub>t <sub>s</sub>li<sub>g</sub>htl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>till id<sub>en</sub>tif<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>even</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Let $w \in \{ 0 , \ldots , W \}$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow.</sub> F<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub> $w ,$ th<sub>e groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erva</sub>l<sub>s are so</sub>ftl<sub>y ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>aroun</sub>d th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es, an</sub>d <sub>range-aware</sub> t<sub>rue-pos</sub>iti<sub>ve,</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>se-</sub> <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ve, an</sub>d <sub>prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on va</sub>l<sub>ues are compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>across score</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>s.</sub> Thi<sub>s y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s a range-aware</sub> ROC curve and a range-aware precision-recall curve for each w. TSB-AD then averages the corresponding <sub>areas across a</sub>ll <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ows:</sub>

$$

$$

TSB-AD also re<sub>p</sub>orts several F1-st<sub>y</sub>le metrics after convertin<sub>g</sub> the anomal<sub>y</sub> score to binar<sub>y p</sub>redictions. Wh<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>searc</sub>h<sub>es</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue.</sub> Th<sub>ese me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs are use</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l f<sub>or</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>or</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e sens</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld selection and, in some cases, to point-adjustment efects.

St<sub>a</sub>nd<sub>a</sub>rd<sub>-</sub>F1<sub>.</sub> St<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>-</sub>F1 i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>armon</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>mean</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>reca</sub>ll<sub>:</sub>

$$

$$

Th<sub>e o</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on repor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>-w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> F1 <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on-reca</sub>ll <sub>curve w</sub>h<sub>en no</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d threshold is <sub>p</sub>rovided. The F-measure itself follows the classical information-retrieval definition (van Rijsbergen, 1979).

PA-F1. Point-adjusted F1 first modifies the binary predictions with an event-level adjustment: if any ti<sub>mes</sub>t<sub>amp</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>id<sub>e a groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erva</sub>l i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s expan</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o cover</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t whole interval. Standard point-wise F1 is then computed on the adjusted prediction vector. PA-F1 <sub>rewar</sub>d<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ng a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>one po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t i<sub>n eac</sub>h l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>even</sub>t<sub>,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t it <sub>can overs</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> f<sub>or no</sub>i<sub>sy</sub> scores that fire anywhere inside many anomalous intervals. This point-adjustment protocol is widely traced to Donut (Xu et al., 2018) and is included b<sub>y</sub> TSB-AD for com<sub>p</sub>leteness (Liu & Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos, 2024). For this reason<sub>,</sub> we do not use PA-F1 as a <sub>p</sub>rimar<sub>y</sub> com<sub>p</sub>arison metric.

E<sub>ven</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d F1<sub>.</sub> E<sub>ven</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d F1 <sub>measures reca</sub>ll <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e anoma</sub>l<sub>y-even</sub>t l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>.</sub> A <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>even</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d if <sub>a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>one pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>ous</sub> ti<sub>mes</sub>t<sub>amp over</sub>l<sub>aps</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>even</sub>t<sub>.</sub> TSB<sub>-</sub>AD <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>nes</sub> thi<sub>s even</sub>t <sub>reca</sub>ll <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>-w</sub>i<sub>se prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> h<sub>armon</sub>i<sub>c mean.</sub> Thi<sub>s me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>s</sub> l<sub>ess sens</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o exac</sub>t <sub>coverage w</sub>ithi<sub>n an even</sub>t th<sub>an</sub> St<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>-</sub>F1<sub>,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t it <sub>s</sub>till d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>s on a</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s even</sub>t<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l famil<sub>y</sub> is used in TSB-AD followin<sub>g p</sub>rior multivariate TSAD evaluation work (Gar<sub>g</sub> et al., 2022; Liu & Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos, 2024).

R<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d F1<sub>.</sub> R<sub>ange-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d F1 <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es over</sub>l<sub>ap</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y ranges an</sub>d <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th anomal<sub>y</sub> ran<sub>g</sub>es. It follows the ran<sub>g</sub>e-based <sub>p</sub>recision and recall framework for time series (Tatbul et al., 2018). The ran<sub>g</sub>e recall used b<sub>y</sub> TSB-AD combines an existence reward and an overla<sub>p</sub> reward:

$$
R _ { \mathrm { r e c a l l } } = \frac { 1 } { | \mathcal { E } | } \sum _ { E \in \mathcal { E } } \left( \alpha \operatorname { E x i s t } ( E , \hat { y } ) + ( 1 - \alpha ) \operatorname { O v e r l a p } ( E , \hat { y } ) \right) ,\tag{8}
$$

where E is the set of ground-truth anomaly ranges and the oficial implementation uses $\alpha = 0 . 2$ . We follow the TSB-AD evaluation <sub>p</sub>rotocol (Liu & Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos, 2024) and additionall<sub>y</sub> include recent <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>pos</sub>td<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l TSB<sub>-</sub>AD t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> B<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow we group eac</sub>h b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> b<sub>y ca</sub>t<sub>egory an</sub>d <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> <sub>a one-paragrap</sub>h d<sub>escr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s are</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>it<sub>e</sub>d <sub>papers.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> f<sub>am</sub>ili<sub>es w</sub>ith b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>per-</sub> <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>su</sub>b<sub>sequence var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> TSB<sub>-</sub>AD’<sub>s</sub> “S<sub>ub</sub>” <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>es a s</sub>lidi<sub>ng-w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow su</sub>b<sub>sequence wrapper</sub> <sub>aroun</sub>d th<sub>e un</sub>d<sub>er</sub>l<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng a</sub>l<sub>gor</sub>ith<sub>m; we c</sub>it<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>paper an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e su</sub>b<sub>sequence var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

## C<sub>.</sub>1 St<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub>

Th<sub>ese me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s pre</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eep</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d t<sub>rea</sub>t <sub>eac</sub>h d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>, or s</sub>lidi<sub>ng su</sub>b<sub>sequence, as an</sub> it<sub>em</sub> i<sub>n a</sub> feature space, scoring by distance, density, isolation, or projection residual. They are interpretable, fast, and on man<sub>y</sub> TSB-AD datasets remain com<sub>p</sub>etitive with dee<sub>p</sub> models (Liu & Pa<sub>p</sub>arrizos, 2024).

IForest / Sub-IForest (Liu et al.<sub>,</sub> 2008). Builds an ensemble of random binar<sub>y</sub> trees and scores each <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>average</sub> <sub>pa</sub>th l<sub>eng</sub>th <sub>nee</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> i<sub>so</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> it<sub>;</sub> <sub>anoma</sub>li<sub>es</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> f<sub>ewer</sub> <sub>sp</sub>lit<sub>s.</sub> S<sub>ub-</sub>IFo<sub>rest</sub> is the TSB-AD subse<sub>q</sub>uence variant.

LOF / Sub-LOF (Breuni<sub>g</sub> et al.<sub>,</sub> 2000). Com<sub>p</sub>ares the local densit<sub>y</sub> of each <sub>p</sub>oint to the densities of its k-nearest neighbours; large ratios flag local outliers. Sub-LOF is the TSB-AD subsequence variant.

Builds an inde<sub>p</sub>endent histo<sub>g</sub>ram <sub>p</sub>er feature <sub>an</sub>d <sub>scores eac</sub>h <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e as</sub> th<sub>e nega</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> l<sub>og o</sub>f th<sub>e pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f bi<sub>n</sub> d<sub>ens</sub>iti<sub>es.</sub> S<sub>ub-</sub>HBOS i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> TSB<sub>-</sub>AD <sub>su</sub>b<sub>sequence var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

A one-class kernel SVM that se<sub>p</sub>arates the trainin<sub>g</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>n</sub> i<sub>n</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure space; s</sub>i<sub>gne</sub>d di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e anoma</sub>l<sub>y score.</sub> S<sub>ub-</sub>OCSVM is the TSB-AD subse<sub>q</sub>uence variant.

MCD / Sub-MCD (Rousseeuw<sub>,</sub> 1984). Fits a robust mean and covariance from the cleanest subset <sub>o</sub>f <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s us</sub>i<sub>ng m</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>mum-covar</sub>i<sub>ance-</sub>d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>nan</sub>t <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> M<sub>a</sub>h<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ano</sub>bi<sub>s</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t fit i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> anomal<sub>y</sub> score. Sub-MCD is the TSB-AD subse<sub>q</sub>uence variant.

Projects onto leadin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rinci<sub>p</sub>al com<sub>p</sub>onents; reconstruction residuals along major and minor components form the anomaly score. Sub-PCA is the TSB-AD subsequence <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Decom<sub>p</sub>oses the data matrix as low-rank <sub>p</sub>lus s<sub>p</sub>arse via <sub>p</sub>rinci<sub>p</sub>al <sub>componen</sub>t <sub>pursu</sub>it<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e sparse componen</sub>t <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> th<sub>e anoma</sub>li<sub>es.</sub>

Fits an em<sub>p</sub>irical co<sub>p</sub>ula on the mar<sub>g</sub>inals and uses tail <sub>p</sub>robabilities of each <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e anoma</sub>l<sub>y score; parame</sub>t<sub>er-</sub>f<sub>ree an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erpre</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub>

Clusters the data, distin<sub>g</sub>uishes “lar<sub>g</sub>e” and “small” clusters, and scores each <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t b<sub>y a we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>e</sub>d di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e neares</sub>t l<sub>arge c</sub>l<sub>us</sub>t<sub>er.</sub>

Slides windows over the series, fits k-means on the windowed <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs, an</sub>d <sub>scores eac</sub>h <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub> b<sub>y</sub> E<sub>uc</sub>lid<sub>ean</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance</sub> t<sub>o</sub> it<sub>s ass</sub>i<sub>gne</sub>d <sub>cen</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>id<sub>;</sub> KM<sub>e</sub>a<sub>ns</sub>AD<sub>\_</sub>U i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>unsuperv</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>compos</sub>it<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>hi<sub>ppe</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith TSB<sub>-</sub>AD<sub>;</sub> <sub>we c</sub>it<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>.</sub>

N<sub>eura</sub>l d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t l<sub>earn a mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>o</sub>f <sub>norma</sub>l b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oenco</sub>d<sub>er recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>s, or con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>ti<sub>ve a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d fl<sub>ag po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s.</sub>

A vanilla feed-forward autoencoder ma<sub>p</sub>s windows to a l<sub>ow-</sub>di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ona</sub>l l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>space;</sub> <sub>per-po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>error</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>score.</sub>

Stochastic recurrent network combinin GRU with a VAE and <sub>p</sub>l<sub>anar</sub> <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> fl<sub>ows;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t’<sub>s</sub> <sub>recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>score,</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>per-c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erpre</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y.</sub>

Two autoencoders share an encoder and are adversariall<sub>y</sub> trained; the <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y score com</sub>bi<sub>nes recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on error</sub> f<sub>rom one</sub> d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>er w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>scr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>or-s</sub>t<sub>y</sub>l<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oss o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>secon</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

A VAE trained on seasonal KPIs with MCMC-based im<sub>p</sub>utation of missin<sub>g</sub> / <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>ous po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s; recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e anoma</sub>l<sub>y score.</sub>

TimesNet (Wu et al.<sub>,</sub> 2023). Resha<sub>p</sub>es a 1-D series into a stack of 2-D tensors based on dominant <sub>per</sub>i<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s,</sub> th<sub>en app</sub>li<sub>es parame</sub>t<sub>er-e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t i<sub>ncep</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ra- an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er-per</sub>i<sub>o</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> jointly. For AD, residual-based scoring is used.

FITS (Xu et al.<sub>,</sub> 2024). A<sub>pp</sub>lies com<sub>p</sub>lex-valued linear inter<sub>p</sub>olation in the fre<sub>q</sub>uenc<sub>y</sub> domain after discarding negligible high-frequency components; matches deep models with roughly 10 k parameters. F<sub>or</sub> AD<sub>, res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s use</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

PatchTST (Nie et al.<sub>,</sub> 2023). Channel-inde<sub>p</sub>endent transformer over <sub>p</sub>atches of the time series, with <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>ng we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s.</sub> O<sub>r</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ll<sub>y a</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t<sub>er; use</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> TSB<sub>-</sub>AD <sub>as a</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t<sub>-res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>anoma</sub>l<sub>y</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or.</sub>

CrossAD (Li et al., 2025). The strongest public multi-scale TSAD method prior to ours. CrossAD generates multi-scale views via average-pooling, then runs a scale-independent encoder whose blockdiagonal attention mask forbids any cross-scale interaction inside the encoder. A separate cross-scale decoder reconstructs each scale from the encodings of strictly coarser scales, privileging the coarse scale as the uni<sub>q</sub>ue conditionin<sub>g</sub> si<sub>g</sub>nal. A cross-window <sub>q</sub>uer<sub>y</sub> librar<sub>y</sub> (<sub>p</sub>eriod-aware router + EMA-u<sub>p</sub>dated global prototypes) injects long-range context. The asymmetric coarse-to-fine information flow is exactly the desi<sub>g</sub>n constraint MSCAD removes.

CATCH (Wu et al.<sub>,</sub> 2025). Channel-aware multivariate detector with fre<sub>q</sub>uenc<sub>y p</sub>atchin<sub>g</sub> and a learned <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l <sub>m</sub>i<sub>xer;</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces a</sub> f<sub>requency-aware pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> t<sub>o cap</sub>t<sub>ure non-s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>onary</sub> b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>-c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>es.</sub>

KAN-AD (Zhou et al.<sub>,</sub> 2025). Reformulates anomal<sub>y</sub> detection with Fourier-basis Kolmo<sub>g</sub>orov–Arnold <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k<sub>s;</sub> d<sub>econs</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e norma</sub>l <sub>pa</sub>tt<sub>ern</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a sum o</sub>f <sub>smoo</sub>th <sub>un</sub>i<sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>as</sub>i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ons, ac</sub>hi<sub>ev</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> strong UTS performance with only ∼10<sup>3</sup> parameters.

18: $\mathbf { r } _ { s } \gets \mathrm { L a y e r N o r m } ( \bar { \mathbf { z } } _ { s } + \mathbf { a } _ { s } )$   
19: $\tilde { \mathbf { z } } _ { s } \gets \mathrm { L a y e r N o r m } ( \mathbf { r } _ { s } + \mathrm { F F N } _ { b } ( \mathbf { r } _ { s } ) )$   
20: <sub>en</sub>d f<sub>or</sub>   
21: ${ \bf z } _ { s } \gets \tilde { { \bf z } } _ { s }$ f<sub>or</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll $s = 1 , \ldots , S$ ▷ synchronous scale update   
22: <sub>en</sub>d f<sub>or</sub>   
23: f<sub>or</sub> $s = 1 , \ldots , S$ d<sub>o</sub> ▷ per-scale decoding   
24: $\hat { \mathbf { u } } _ { s } \gets \mathrm { D e c } _ { s } ( \mathbf { z } _ { s } )$   
25: $\begin{array} { r } { e _ { s }  \frac { 1 } { N _ { s } P _ { s } } \sum _ { j = 0 } ^ { \tilde { N } _ { s } - 1 } \| \hat { \mathbf { u } } _ { s } ^ { ( j ) } - \mathbf { u } _ { s } ^ { ( j ) } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } } \end{array}$   
26: <sub>en</sub>d f<sub>or</sub>   
27: return $\begin{array} { r } { f _ { \theta } ( \mathbf { x } ) \gets \frac { 1 } { S } \sum _ { s = 1 } ^ { S } e _ { s } } \end{array}$   
28<sub>: en</sub>d f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>   
29: $\theta ^ { \star }  \theta ; \ell ^ { \star }  + \infty ; t  0$ ▷ train with early stopping on $\nu$   
30: for e<sub>p</sub>och $= 1 , \ldots , E$ d<sub>o</sub>   
31: Update θ on minibatches of size b: Adam(η), grad-norm clipped at 1.0, cosine LR schedule.   
32: $\ell \dot { \mathbf { \xi } }  \mathbb { E } _ { \mathbf { x } \in \mathcal { V } } [ f _ { \theta } ( \mathbf { x } ) ]$   
33: if $\ell < \ell ^ { \star }$ th<sub>en</sub> $\tilde { \theta ^ { \star } }  \theta ; \ell ^ { \star }  \ell ; t  0$   
34: <sub>e</sub>l<sub>se</sub> $t \gets t + 1$   
35: <sub>en</sub>d if   
36: if $\mid t \geq \rho$ th<sub>en</sub> b<sub>rea</sub>k   
37: <sub>en</sub>d $\mathbf { \bar { i f } } ^ { \prime }$   
38: end for   
39: return $f _ { \theta ^ { \star } }$

keeps the bridge cost fixed in S: scaling from S=3 to S=4 would add only the per-scale embedding / <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> / d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>er, no</sub>t b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge parame</sub>t<sub>ers.</sub>

We re<sub>p</sub>ort (i) the full TSB-AD baseline com<sub>p</sub>arison on both s<sub>p</sub>lits at the leaderboard’s native <sub>p</sub>recision (§E.1), (ii) <sub>p</sub>er-seed mean±standard deviation across all ei<sub>g</sub>ht metrics tracked b<sub>y</sub> TSB-AD’s metric suite (§E.2), (iii) a <sub>p</sub>erd<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t b<sub>rea</sub>kd<sub>own o</sub>f MSCAD’<sub>s per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> t<sub>o ma</sub>k<sub>e per-</sub>d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n s</sub>t<sub>reng</sub>th<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>wea</sub>k<sub>nesses</sub> i<sub>nspec</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> (§E.3), (iv) com<sub>p</sub>ute and <sub>p</sub>arameter costs (§E.4), and (v) <sub>q</sub>ualitative case studies of MSCAD detections on re<sub>p</sub>resentative series (§E.5). The headline numbers in §4.2 are seed-a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ated means on six metrics. Table 11 re<sub>p</sub>orts the full eight-metric breakdown—the six already shown in Table 1 plus PA-F1 and Afiliation-F—together with the per-seed standard deviation across seeds {2026, 2027, 2028}. Two observations are immediate. ## E<sub>.</sub>3 P<sub>er-</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t b<sub>rea</sub>kd<sub>own</sub>

Table 10 reports the same six metrics on each of the 40 TSB-AD source datasets (23 univariate, 17 multivariate). The breakdown surfaces three patterns. (a) Strong performance on long-context industrial telemetry. MSCAD scores VUS-PR ≥ 0.78 on Exathlon, OPPORTUNITY, SMD, and SVDB on the univariate split, and ≥ 0.60 on Exathlon, MSL, and SVDB on the multivariate split, all of which contain mixtures of point and contextual anomalies that exercise the multi-scale design. (b) Dificulty on short, near-zeroanomaly-ratio series. Single-series subsets such as Power, Stock, TAO, and YAHOO on UTS, and GHL, GECCO<sub>,</sub> OPPORTUNITY<sub>,</sub> and Genesis on MTS<sub>,</sub> account for almost all of MSCAD’s <sub>p</sub>er-dataset losses. Th<sub>ese ser</sub>i<sub>es</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> f<sub>ew or no anoma</sub>l<sub>y even</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> h<sub>eavy c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub> i<sub>m</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ance, an</sub>d li<sub>m</sub>it<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> l<sub>eng</sub>th<sub>, con</sub>diti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>enge any recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d f<sub>rom scra</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e per-ser</sub>i<sub>es</sub> amortization direction discussed in Appendix H. (c) Robustness under domain shift. MSCAD remains within 0.03 VUS-PR of the within-split mean on diverse domains: medical waveforms (LTDB, MITDB, SVDB), s<sub>p</sub>acecraft telemetr<sub>y</sub> (SMAP, MSL), web trafic (NAB, IOPS, YAHOO), and human-activit<sub>y</sub> sensin<sub>g</sub> (Daphnet, OPPORTUNITY). This robustness is delivered by a singlefixed configuration: the per-dataset t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>exac</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> <sub>use</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er.</sub>

## E.4 Com<sub>p</sub>ute and <sub>p</sub>arameter cost

T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 12 <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>s parame</sub>t<sub>er coun</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>per-ser</sub>i<sub>es wa</sub>ll ti<sub>mes</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>dli<sub>ne</sub> MSCAD <sub>an</sub>d t<sub>wo re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> points: a smaller d=128, B=1 MSCAD variant and the same headline architecture with the bridge removed (B=0). Three points are worth noting. (a) Most of the parameter budget sits in the per-scale branches (5.18M of 5.97M, or ∼87%); the bridge itself adds only ∼13%. The small fraction of parameters contributed by the bridge underscores that the gain reported in Table 2 is driven by the interaction, not by additional capacity. (b) Removing the bridge (no-bridge row) cuts the per-seed wall time by roughly 55%, from 24.8 h to 11.2 h, while the parameter count shrinks by only 13%. The compute cost of the b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y ac</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on memory an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on qua</sub>d<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>c over conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t tokens, not by additional weights. (c) The headline configuration’s per-series wall time is 14 s on UTS files and 7.8 min on MTS files; the MTS overhead is linear in the number of channels because each channel is <sub>p</sub>rocessed inde<sub>p</sub>endentl<sub>y</sub> (a deliberate TSB-AD-<sub>p</sub>rotocol choice for fair com<sub>p</sub>arison across detectors with and without ex<sub>p</sub>licit channel modellin<sub>g</sub>). ## F.4 Brid<sub>g</sub>e o<sub>p</sub>erator

T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 16 <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>aces</sub> th<sub>e cross-a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on opera</sub>t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>id<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e symme</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge w</sub>ith th<sub>ree parame</sub>t<sub>er-</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>aggrega</sub>t<sub>ors — mean-, sum-, an</sub>d <sub>max-poo</sub>l <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens —</sub> h<sub>o</sub>ldi<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> FFN+residual wrapper, the symmetric query-context construction, and the bridge depth B=2 fixed. Two takeaways. (i) Every non-trivial operator beats the no-bridge / identity baselines by a comparable margin (+0.026 to +0.031 All VUS-PR over no-bridge), confirming again that the load-bearing component is the symmetric exchange, not the choice of operator: the FFN+residual wrapper alone (identity) lifts All by only +0.002, whereas any genuine cross-scale aggregation lifts it by +0.027 or more. (ii) Cross-attention is the strongest aggregator at the headline: it wins All VUS-PR (0.5356) and MTS VUS-PR (0.4705), edges out max-pool by +0.004 All, beats mean-pool by +0.003 All, and beats sum-pool by +0.004 All despite sum’s slightly higher UTS number (0.5702); sum’s strong UTS is ofset by a much larger MTS standard deviation (±0.031, vs. ±0.002–0.004 for the others), reflecting one outlier seed on multivariate. We th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>au</sub>lt t<sub>o cross-a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge opera</sub>t<sub>or.</sub> Th<sub>e mean-poo</sub>l <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns a</sub>tt<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>ve as a</sub> parameter-light alternative (−0.79 M parameters) for compute-constrained deployments and is what we <sub>use</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>ge-</sub>di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 3<sub>.</sub>
